One of many protests in Beirut

The Parliament is elected for a four-year term by popular vote on the basis of sectarian proportional representation.[^cite_note-cia2-4] The executive branch consists of the President, the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state), and the Prime Minister, the [head of government](37570.md). The parliament elects the president for a non-renewable six-year term by a two-thirds majority. The president appoints the Prime Minister,[^cite_note-234] following consultations with the parliament. The president and the prime minister form a cabinet, which must also adhere to the sectarian distribution set out by confessionalism.&#x20;

In an unprecedented move, the Lebanese parliament has extended its own term twice amid protests, the last being on 5 November 2014,[^cite_note-235] an act which comes in direct contradiction with democracy and article #42 of the Lebanese constitution as no elections have taken place.[^cite_note-constitution-10] Lebanon was without a President between May 2014 and October 2016.[^cite_note-Aoun-236][^cite_note-237] Nationwide elections were finally scheduled for [May 2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Lebanese_general_election).[^cite_note-238] As of August 2019, the Lebanese cabinet included two ministers directly affiliated with [Hezbollah](13919.md), in addition to a close but officially non-member minister.[^cite_note-239] The most recent parliamentary [elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Lebanese_general_election) were held on 15 May 2022.[^cite_note-240]&#x20;

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Governorates of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governorates_of_Lebanon), [Districts of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Lebanon), and [Municipalities of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipalities_of_Lebanon)

Lebanon is divided into nine [governorates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governorate) (*muḥāfaẓāt*, [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): محافظات; singular *[muḥāfaẓah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhafazah)*, [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): محافظة) which are further subdivided into twenty-five districts (*aqdyah*, [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): أقضية; singular: *[qadāʾ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qadaa)* [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): قضاء).[^cite_note-241] The districts themselves are also divided into several municipalities, each enclosing a group of cities or villages. ### Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Lebanon)



United Nations Lebanon headquarters in Beirut

Lebanon concluded negotiations on an association agreement with the European Union in late 2001, and both sides initialed the accord in January 2002. It is included in the European Union's [European Neighbourhood Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Neighbourhood_Policy) (ENP), which aims at bringing the EU and its neighbours closer. Lebanon also has bilateral trade agreements with several Arab states and is working toward accession to the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization).&#x20;

Lebanon enjoys good relations with virtually all of the other Arab countries (despite historic tensions with [Libya](17633.md) and [Syria](7515849.md)), and hosted an [Arab League](52625.md) Summit in [March 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Arab_League_summit) for the first time in over 35 years. Lebanon is a member of the [Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francophonie) countries and hosted the Francophonie Summit in October 2002 as well as the [Jeux de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeux_de_la_Francophonie) in [2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Jeux_de_la_Francophonie).&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Lebanese Armed Forces](17779.md)



Soldiers of the Lebanese army, 2009

The [Lebanese Armed Forces (LAF)](17779.md) has 72,000 active personnel,[^cite_note-Lebanon-242] including 1,100 in the air force, and 1,000 in the navy.[^cite_note-Stinson-243] The Lebanese Armed Forces' primary missions include defending Lebanon and its citizens against external aggression, maintaining internal stability and security, confronting threats against the country's vital interests, engaging in social development activities, and undertaking relief operations in coordination with public and humanitarian institutions.[^cite_note-lafmission-244]&#x20;

Lebanon is a major recipient of foreign military aid.[^cite_note-245] With over \$400 million since 2005, it is the second largest per capita recipient of [American military aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_military_aid) behind Israel.[^cite_note-246]&#x20;

The LAF is considered less powerful and influential than [Hezbollah](13919.md) in Lebanon. Hezbollah claims to have around 100,000 personnel, including 20,000 active fighters and 20,000 in reserves and is supplied with advanced weaponry, including rockets and drones from [Iran](14653.md).[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-CFR-2020-248] It possesses "a larger arsenal of artillery than most nations enjoy."[^cite_note-CFR-2020-248] A report from the [Center for Strategic and International Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Strategic_and_International_Studies) in 2018 called it "the world's most heavily armed non-state actor."[^cite_note-CFR-2020-248] In June 2024, experts estimated that Hezbollah had 150,000–200,000 rockets and missiles of various ranges.[^cite_note-CFR-2020-248]&#x20;

Hezbollah effectively controls large portions of southern Lebanon, and has greater [military strength](6222202.md) than the Lebanese armed forces.[^cite_note-249] The government of Lebanon has been unable or unwilling to prevent Hezbollah attacks on Israel, and violent conflict between Israel and Hezbollah in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-CFR-2020-248] Many Islamist and Palestinian militias operate in refugee camps because of an agreement that prevents the Lebanese Army from entering them. Many people wanted by the Lebanese government are believed to have taken refuge in the camp as a result of the lack of Lebanese authority.&#x20;

### Law



[Grand Serail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Serail) from Riad El Solh Square, Beirut

There are 18 officially recognized religious groups in Lebanon, each with its own [family law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_law_in_Lebanon) legislation and set of religious courts.[^cite_note-unesco1-250] The Lebanese legal system is based on the [French system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_France), and is a [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) country, with the exception for matters related to personal status (succession, marriage, divorce, adoption, etc.), which are governed by a separate set of laws designed for each sectarian community. For instance, the Islamic personal status laws are inspired by the [Sharia](28840.md) law.[^cite_note-globalex-251] For Muslims, these tribunals deal with questions of marriage, divorce, custody, and inheritance and wills. For non-Muslims, personal status jurisdiction is split: the law of inheritance and wills falls under national civil jurisdiction, while Christian and Jewish religious courts are competent for marriage, divorce, and custody. Catholics can additionally appeal before the [Vatican Rota court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Rota).[^cite_note-252]&#x20;

The most notable set of codified laws is the Code des Obligations et des Contrats promulgated in 1932 and equivalent to the [French Civil Code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Civil_Code).[^cite_note-globalex-251] [Capital punishment](5902.md) was abolished in 2026; Lebanon is the only Arab country to not retain the death penalty.[^cite_note-globalex-251] The Lebanese court system consists of three levels: courts of first instance, courts of appeal, and the court of cassation. The Constitutional Council rules on constitutionality of laws and electoral frauds. There also is a system of religious courts having jurisdiction over personal status matters within their own communities, with rules on matters such as marriage and inheritance.[^cite_note-Carnegie-253]&#x20;

In 1990, article 95 was amended to provide that the parliament shall take necessary measures to abolish political structure based on religious affiliation, but that until such time only the highest positions in public civil service, including the judiciary, military, security forces, public and mixed institutions, shall be divided equally between [Christians](18337522.md) and [Muslims](19541.md) without regard to the denominational affiliation within each community.[^cite_note-254]&#x20;

In August 2026, the government of Lebanon abolished the death penalty.[^cite_note-255]&#x20;

#### LGBT rights

Main article: [LGBTQ rights in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Lebanon)

Male homosexuality is illegal in Lebanon.[^cite_note-256] Discrimination against [LGBTQ people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_in_Lebanon) in Lebanon is widespread.[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] According to 2019 survey by the Pew Research Center, 85% of Lebanese respondents believe that [homosexuality](5488304.md) should not be accepted by society.[^cite_note-pew2020-259]&#x20;

A gender and sexuality conference, held annually in Lebanon, since 2013, was moved abroad in 2019 after a religious group on Facebook called for the organizers' arrest and the cancellation of the conference for "inciting immorality." General Security Forces shut down the 2018 conference and indefinitely denied non-Lebanese LGBT activists who attended the conference permission to re-enter the country.[^cite_note-260]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Lebanon)



[Beirut Central District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beirut_Central_District)

[Lebanon's constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Lebanon) states that 'the economic system is free and ensures private initiative and the right to private property'. Lebanon's economy follows a [laissez-faire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laissez-faire) model.[^cite_note-expus-261] Most of the economy is [dollarized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dollarized), and the country has no restrictions on the movement of capital across its borders.[^cite_note-expus-261] The Lebanese government's intervention in foreign trade is minimal.[^cite_note-expus-261] The [Investment Development Authority of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investment_Development_Authority_of_Lebanon) was established with the aim of promoting investment in Lebanon. In 2001, Investment Law No.360[^cite_note-262] was enacted to reinforce the organisation's mission.&#x20;

Lebanon is now suffering the worst [economic crisis](63804918.md) in decades.[^cite_note-The_Diplomat-2020-156][^cite_note-Sly-2020-157] As of 2023, the GDP has shrunk by 40% since 2018, and the currency has experienced a significant depreciation of 95%.[^cite_note-263] The annual inflation rate exceeds 200%, rendering the minimum wage equivalent to approximately \$1 per day.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2022-264] This was the first time Lebanon had devalued its official exchange rate in 25 years.[^cite_note-Aljazeera-2023-175] According to the United Nations, three out of every four Lebanese individuals fall below the poverty line.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2022-264] The crisis stems from a long-term [Ponzi scheme](47185.md) by the [Central Bank of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Banque_du_Liban), borrowing dollars at high interest rates to sustain deficits and maintain a currency peg. By 2019, insufficient new deposits led to an unsustainable situation, resulting in weeks-long bank closures, arbitrary capital controls, and ultimately, the country's default in 2020.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2023-265]&#x20;

Throughout the Ottoman and French mandatory periods and into the 1960s, Lebanon experienced prosperity, serving as a hub for banking, financial services, and a key distribution center for the Middle East. The local economy thrived with a foundation in industries related to food processing, clothing, jewelry, and carpets. This prosperity was later marred by four decades of conflict.[^cite_note-Cohen-2015-225] Following the end of the civil war, Lebanon has developed a service-based economy centered around finance, real estate, and tourism.[^cite_note-266] Nearly 65% of the Lebanese workforce attain employment in the services sector.[^cite_note-workforce-267] The GDP contribution, accordingly, amounts to roughly 67.3% of the annual Lebanese GDP.[^cite_note-dos-2010-03-22-21] However, dependence on the tourism and banking sectors leaves the economy vulnerable to political instability.[^cite_note-Canada-268]&#x20;

The urban population in Lebanon is noted for its commercial enterprise.[^cite_note-269] Emigration has yielded Lebanese "commercial networks" throughout the world.[^cite_note-270] In 2008, Remittances from Lebanese abroad totalled \$8.2 billion[^cite_note-271] and account for one-fifth of the country's economy.[^cite_note-272] In 2005, Lebanon had the largest proportion of skilled labor among Arab States.[^cite_note-273]&#x20;

### Agriculture

The [agricultural sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture_in_Lebanon) in Lebanon employs 20–25% of the total [workforce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workforce),[^cite_note-The_Economist-2020-274] and contributed 3.1% to the country's GDP,[^cite_note-275] as of 2020. Lebanon has the highest proportion of cultivable land in the Arab world.[^cite_note-agriculture-276] Major crops include apples, peaches, oranges, and lemons.[^cite_note-dos-2010-03-22-21] A significant portion of the country's factories, approximately one-third, is dedicated to producing packaged food items, ranging from poultry to pickles.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2020-274] However, despite favorable conditions for farming and diverse microclimates, the country depends on food imports, constituting 80% of its consumption. This is mainly attributed to the small scale of many farms, preventing the benefits of economies of scale.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2020-274] The ongoing economic crisis and devaluation of the Lebanese pound have also negatively impacted the agricultural sector, particularly through elevated costs for essential imports such as seeds and fertilizers. This economic strain compounds existing burdens for farmers, including escalating debts and inefficient agricultural practices. Consequently, farmers are observing a decline in revenues and encountering difficulties in meeting loan repayment obligations.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2020-274][^cite_note-277]&#x20;

The [commodities market](48190.md) in Lebanon includes substantial [gold coin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_coin) production, however according to [International Air Transport Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Air_Transport_Association) (IATA) standards, they must be declared upon exportation to any foreign country.[^cite_note-278]&#x20;

### Manufacturing and industry

Industry in Lebanon is mainly limited to small businesses that reassemble and package imported parts. In 2004, industry ranked second in workforce, with 26% of the Lebanese working population,[^cite_note-workforce-267] and second in GDP contribution, with 21% of Lebanon's GDP.[^cite_note-dos-2010-03-22-21]&#x20;

[Oil](23195.md) has recently been discovered inland and in the seabed between Lebanon, Cyprus, Israel and Egypt and talks are underway between [Cyprus](5593.md) and [Egypt](8087628.md) to reach an agreement regarding the exploration of these resources. The seabed separating Lebanon and Cyprus is believed to hold significant quantities of crude oil and natural gas.[^cite_note-279] On 10 May 2013, the Lebanese minister of energy and water clarified that seismic images of the Lebanese sea bed are undergoing detailed explanation of their contents and that up till now, approximately 10% have been covered. Preliminary inspection of the results showed, with over 50% probability, that 10% of Lebanon's exclusive economic zone held up to 660 million barrels of oil and up to 30×10<sup>12</sup> cu ft of gas.[^cite_note-280]&#x20;

Lebanon has a [significant drug industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_economy_in_Lebanon), including both production and trade. Western intelligence estimate an annual production of over 4 million pounds of [hashish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis_in_Lebanon) and 20,000 pounds of [heroin](14034.md), generating profits exceeding \$4 billion. In recent decades, Hezbollah has intensified its engagement in the drug economy, with narcotics serving as a significant revenue stream for the group. Despite some of the harvest being retained for local use, a significant amount is smuggled worldwide. Despite ongoing efforts, the government's inability to control the drug-producing Beqaa Valley and address illicit [Captagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fenethylline) factories allows for the persistent occurrence of drug trades, impacting Lebanon's economy and regional stability.[^cite_note-Arab_News-2021-281][^cite_note-egic-2023-282][^cite_note-Cwerman-2024-283]&#x20;

### Science and technology



[Saint Joseph University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph_University_of_Beirut)'s Campus of Innovation and Sports on Damascus Street, [Beirut](37428.md)

Lebanon was ranked 90th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-284][^cite_note-285] Notable scientists from Lebanon include [Hassan Kamel Al-Sabbah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Kamel_Al-Sabbah), [Rammal Rammal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rammal_Rammal), and [Edgar Choueiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edgar_Choueiri).[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]&#x20;

In 1960, a science club from a university in Beirut started a Lebanese space program called "[the Lebanese Rocket Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lebanese_Rocket_Society)". They achieved great success until 1966 where the program was stopped because of both war and external pressure.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289]&#x20;

### Development

&#x20;In the 1950s, GDP growth was the second highest in the world. Despite having no oil reserves, Lebanon, as the Arab world's banking center[^cite_note-290] and among its trading center, had a high national income.[^cite_note-291]



Lebanese real GDP 1970–2017

&#x20;The [1975–1990 civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War) heavily damaged Lebanon's economic infrastructure,[^cite_note-Stinson-243] cut national output by half, and all but ended Lebanon's position as a West Asian [entrepôt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrep%C3%B4t) and banking hub.[^cite_note-cia2-4] The subsequent period of relative peace enabled the central government to restore control in [Beirut](37428.md), begin collecting taxes, and regain access to key port and government facilities. Economic recovery has been helped by a financially sound banking system and resilient small- and medium-scale manufacturers, with family remittances, banking services, manufactured and farm exports, and international aid as the main sources of foreign exchange.[^cite_note-cia2001-292]



Port of Beirut

Until July 2006, Lebanon enjoyed considerable stability, Beirut's reconstruction was almost complete,[^cite_note-293] and increasing numbers of tourists poured into the nation's resorts.[^cite_note-tourism-294] The economy witnessed growth, with bank assets reaching over 75 billion US dollars.[^cite_note-economy-stat-295] [Market capitalization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_capitalization) was also at an all-time high, estimated at \$10.9 billion at the end of the second quarter of 2006.[^cite_note-economy-stat-295] The month-long [2006 war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War) severely damaged Lebanon's fragile economy, especially the tourism sector. According to a preliminary report published by the Lebanese [Ministry of Finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Finance_\(Lebanon\)) on 30 August 2006, a major economic decline was expected as a result of the fighting.[^cite_note-296]&#x20;

Over the course of 2008 Lebanon rebuilt its infrastructure mainly in the real estate and tourism sectors, resulting in a comparatively robust post war economy. Major contributors to the reconstruction of Lebanon include [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) (with US\$1.5 billion pledged),[^cite_note-297] the European Union (with about \$1 billion)[^cite_note-298] and a few other Persian Gulf countries with contributions of up to \$800 million.[^cite_note-299]&#x20;

### Tourism



[Beirut](37428.md) is the tourism hub of the country.

Main article: [Tourism in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Lebanon)

The tourism industry accounts for about 10% of [GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP).[^cite_note-lebtourecon-300] Lebanon attracted around 1,333,000 tourists in 2008, thus placing it as 79th out of 191 countries.[^cite_note-301] In 2009, *The New York Times* ranked Beirut the No. 1 travel destination worldwide due to its nightlife and hospitality.[^cite_note-302] In January 2010, the [Ministry of Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Tourism_\(Lebanon\)) announced that 1,851,081 tourists had visited Lebanon in 2009, a 39% increase from 2008.[^cite_note-lebanon-tourism1-303] In 2009, Lebanon hosted the largest number of tourists to date, eclipsing the previous record set before the [Lebanese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War).[^cite_note-ABC_2009_Tourism_Record-304] Tourist arrivals reached two million in 2010, but fell by 37% for the first 10 months of 2012, a decline caused by the war in neighbouring Syria.[^cite_note-lebtourecon-300]&#x20;

In 2011, [Saudi Arabia](349303.md), Jordan, and Japan were the three most popular origin countries of foreign tourists to Lebanon.[^cite_note-305] In summer, a considerable number of visitors to Lebanon consists of Lebanese expatriates coming to visit their hometowns.[^cite_note-The_Economist-2023-265] In 2012, it was reported that an influx of Japanese tourists had caused a rise in popularity of [Japanese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_cuisine) in Lebanon.[^cite_note-306]&#x20;

On 4 May, the United Arab Emirates (UAE) has announced that it will lift its travel ban on Lebanon, effective 7 May 2025. This decision follows a meeting between Lebanese President [Joseph Aoun](53467128.md) and UAE President [Sheikh Mohamed bin Zayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Mohamed_Bin_Zayed) in [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md), during which both leaders agreed to facilitate travel and improve movement between the two countries.[^cite_note-307]&#x20;

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Lebanon)

See also: [Lebanese people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people)

Lebanon had an estimated population of 5,592,631 in 2021, with the number of [Lebanese nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people) estimated at 4,680,212 in July;[^cite_note-UN_WPP-308][^cite_note-UN_WPP_2022-309] however, no official census has been conducted since 1932 due to the sensitive [confessional political balance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confessionalism_\(politics\)) between Lebanon's various religious groups.[^cite_note-310] Identifying all Lebanese as ethnically [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab) is a widely employed example of [panethnicity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panethnicity), as the Lebanese "are descended from many different peoples who are either indigenous, or have occupied, invaded, or settled this corner of the world", making Lebanon, "a mosaic of closely interrelated cultures".[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-Stokes,_Jamie_page_406-313]&#x20;

The [fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertility_rate) fell from 5.00 in 1971 to 1.75 in 2004. Fertility rates vary considerably among the different religious groups: in 2004, it was 2.10 for [Shiites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islam_in_Lebanon), 1.76 for [Sunnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sunni_Muslims) and 1.61 for [Maronites](39667585.md).[^cite_note-demo-reality-314]&#x20;



[Beirut](37428.md), located on the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md), is the most populous city in Lebanon.

[Lebanese people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people) have a long history of emigration stretching back to the 19th century; including partial ancestry, more Lebanese live outside the country than in Lebanon itself. The country has experienced a series of migration waves since independence: over 1.8 million people emigrated from the country between 1975 and 2011.[^cite_note-demo-reality-314] Consequently, millions of [people of Lebanese descent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_diaspora) are spread throughout the world, especially in [Latin America](18524.md);[^cite_note-315] [Brazil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Brazilians) and [Argentina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Argentines) have the largest Lebanese communities,[^cite_note-316] with the former having the largest population in the world, at 5–7 million. North America likewise hosts a large diaspora, particularly in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Canadians) (approximately 250,000–700,000) and the United States (around 2 million).[^cite_note-317] Australia is home to over 270,000 [Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Australian).[^cite_note-318]&#x20;

Large numbers of Lebanese migrated to [West Africa](67393.md),[^cite_note-319] particularly to the [Ivory Coast](57744.md) (100,000)[^cite_note-320] and [Senegal](27278.md) ([30,000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people_in_Senegal)).[^cite_note-321] The Persian Gulf hosts many Lebanese expatriates,[^cite_note-322] led by Saudi Arabia with 269,000 Lebanese citizens.[^cite_note-323] Around a third of the Lebanese workforce, about 350,000, reportedly live in Gulf countries according to some sources.[^cite_note-324] Over 50% of the Lebanese diaspora are Christian, partly due to the large period of Christian emigration before 1943.[^cite_note-325]&#x20;

Lebanon has the largest number of refugees per capita in the world.[^cite_note-Lebanon-2024-326] As of 2024,[^cite_note-327] Lebanon hosted over 1.6 million [refugees](45547.md) and [asylum](1621432.md) seekers: 449,957 from [Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinians_in_Lebanon),[^cite_note-cia2-4] 100,000 from [Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqis_in_Lebanon),[^cite_note-328] over 1.1 million from [Syria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrians_in_Lebanon),[^cite_note-cia2-4][^cite_note-329] and at least 4,000 from [Sudan](27421.md).[^cite_note-330] According to the Economic and Social Commission for Western Asia of the United Nations, among the Syrian refugees, 71% live in poverty and 80% lack legal residency.[^cite_note-chronicle.fanack.com-331] Other estimates put the Syrian refugee population at 1.5 million in 2024,[^cite_note-Lebanon-2024-326] compared to over 1.25 million according to a 2013 estimate by the United Nations.[^cite_note-refugees-136]&#x20;

In the last three decades, lengthy and destructive [armed conflicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_conflict) have ravaged the country. The majority of Lebanese have been affected by armed conflict; those with direct personal experience include 75% of the population, and most others report suffering a range of hardships. In total, almost the entire population (96%) has been affected in some way – either personally or because of the wider consequences of armed conflict.[^cite_note-332]&#x20;

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Lebanon)

See also: [Islam in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lebanon), [Christianity in Lebanon](4796145.md), [Irreligion in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_Lebanon), and [Secularism in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularism_in_Lebanon)



Distribution of religious groups of Lebanon according to 2009 municipal election data with the boundaries of ancient [Mount Lebanon Mutasarrifate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Lebanon_Mutasarrifate) marked with a dotted line.

[^cite_note-333]

Lebanon is the most religiously diverse country in West Asia and the Mediterranean.[^cite_note-334] Because the relative sizes of different religions and religious sects remains a sensitive issue, a national census has not been conducted since 1932.[^cite_note-freedom-335] There are 18 state-recognized religious sects: four [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lebanon), 12 [Christian](4796145.md), one [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_in_Lebanon), and one [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Jews).[^cite_note-freedom-335] The Lebanese government counts its [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze) citizens as part of its [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) population,[^cite_note-DruzeAreCountedAsMuslimsInLebanon-336] although most [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze) today do not identify as [Muslims](19541.md).[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338]&#x20;

It is believed that there has been a decline in the ratio of Christians to Muslims over the past 60 years, due to higher emigration rates of Christians, and a higher birth rate in the Muslim population.[^cite_note-freedom-335] When the last census was held in 1932, Christians made up 53% of Lebanon's population.[^cite_note-demo-reality-314] In 1956, it was estimated that the population was 54% Christian and 44% Muslim.[^cite_note-demo-reality-314]&#x20;

A demographic study conducted by the research firm Statistics Lebanon found that approximately 27% of the population was [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sunni_Muslims), 27% [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims), 21% [Maronite](39667585.md), 8% [Greek Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Christianity_in_Lebanon), 5% [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_in_Lebanon), 5% [Melkite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkite_Christianity_in_Lebanon), and 1% [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_Lebanon), with the remaining 6% mostly belonging to smaller non-native to Lebanon Christian denominations.[^cite_note-freedom-335] The [CIA World Factbook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Factbook) estimates (2020) the following (data does not include Lebanon's sizable Syrian and Palestinian refugee populations): [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lebanon) 67.8% (31.9% [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sunni_Muslims), 31.2% [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims), smaller percentages of [Alawites](436616.md) and [Ismailis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isma%27ilism)), [Christian](4796145.md) 32.4% ([Maronite Catholics](66391078.md) are the largest Christian group), [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Druze_in_Lebanon) 4.5%, and very small numbers of [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jews_in_Lebanon), [Baha'is](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baha%27i_Faith_in_Lebanon), [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_Lebanon), and [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Lebanon).[^cite_note-cia2-4] Other sources like [Euronews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euronews)[^cite_note-339] or the [Madrid-based](41188263.md) diary *[La Razón](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Raz%C3%B3n_\(Madrid\))*[^cite_note-340] estimate the percentage of Christians to be around 53%. A study based on voter registration numbers shows that by 2011, the Christian population was stable compared to that of previous years, making up 34.35% of the population; Muslims, the Druze included, were 65.47% of the population.[^cite_note-341] The [World Values Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Values_Survey) of 2014 put the percentage of [atheists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) in Lebanon at 3.3%.[^cite_note-WVS-342] Survey data indicates a decrease in religious faith within Lebanon, especially noticeable among young people.[^cite_note-343]&#x20;

The [Sunni residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sunni_Muslims) primarily live in Western Beirut, the Southern coast of Lebanon, and [Northern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Lebanon).[^cite_note-LOC_glossary-18] The [Shi'a residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims) primarily live in Southern Beirut, the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md), and [Southern Lebanon](4205968.md).[^cite_note-LOC_glossary-18] The [Maronite Catholic residents](39667585.md) primarily live in Eastern Beirut and around [Mount Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Lebanon).[^cite_note-LOC_glossary-18] The [Greek Orthodox residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Greek_Orthodox_Christians) primarily live in the [Koura region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koura_District), Akkar, Metn, and Beirut ([Achrafieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Achrafieh)).[^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345] The [Melkite Catholic residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Melkite_Christians) live mainly in Beirut, on the eastern slopes of the Lebanon mountains, and in [Zahlé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zahl%C3%A9).[^cite_note-346] The [Druze residents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Druze) are concentrated in the rural, mountainous areas east and south of Beirut.&#x20;

### Language

Main article: [Languages of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Lebanon)

Article 11 of Lebanon's Constitution states that "Arabic is the official national language. A law determines the cases in which the [French language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language) is to be used".[^cite_note-article_11-1] The majority of Lebanese people speak [Lebanese Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Arabic), which is grouped in a larger category called [Levantine Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levantine_Arabic), while [Modern Standard Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic) is mostly used in magazines, newspapers, and formal broadcast media. [Lebanese Sign Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sign_Language) is the language of the Deaf community.&#x20;

There is also significant presence [of French](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_language_in_Lebanon), and [of English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language_in_Lebanon). Almost 40% of Lebanese are considered francophone, and another 15% "partial francophone", and 70% of Lebanon's secondary schools use French as a second language of instruction.[^cite_note-NadeauBarlow2008-347] By comparison, English is used as a secondary language in 30% of Lebanon's secondary schools.[^cite_note-NadeauBarlow2008-347] The use of French is a legacy of France's historic ties to the region, including its [League of Nations mandate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Nations_mandate) over Lebanon following World War I; as of 2005, some 20% of the population used French on a daily basis.[^cite_note-Britannica2-348] The use of Arabic by Lebanon's educated youth is declining, as they usually prefer to speak in French and, to a lesser extent, English, which are seen as more fashionable.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350]&#x20;

English is increasingly used in science and business interactions.[^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352] [Lebanese citizens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_nationality_law) of [Armenian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenians_in_Lebanon), [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeks_in_Lebanon), or [Assyrian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrians_in_Lebanon) descent often speak their ancestral languages with varying degrees of fluency. As of 2009, there were around 150,000 Armenians in Lebanon, or around 5% of the population.[^cite_note-353]&#x20;

### Education

Main article: [Education in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Lebanon)



The Innovation and Sports Campus of [Saint Joseph University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph_University)

According to surveys from the World Economic Forum's 2013 Global Information Technology Report, Lebanon has been ranked globally as the fourth best country for math and science education, and as the tenth best overall for quality of education. In quality of management schools, the country was ranked 13th worldwide.[^cite_note-354]&#x20;

In 2008, the United Nations assigned Lebanon an education index of 0.871. The index, which is determined by the adult literacy rate and the combined primary, secondary, and tertiary gross enrollment ratio, ranked Lebanon 88th out of the 177 countries participating.[^cite_note-undp-355] All Lebanese schools are required to follow a prescribed curriculum designed by the [Ministry of Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Education_and_Higher_Education_\(Lebanon\)). Some of the 1,400 private schools offer [IB programs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IB_program),[^cite_note-356] and may add more courses to their curriculum with approval from the Ministry of Education. The first eight years of education are compulsory by law.[^cite_note-dos-2010-03-22-21]&#x20;

Lebanon has forty-one nationally accredited universities, several of which are internationally recognized.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] The [American University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Beirut) (AUB) and the [Saint Joseph University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Joseph_University) (USJ) were the first Anglophone and the first Francophone universities to open in Lebanon, respectively.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360]&#x20;

Universities in Lebanon, both public and private, largely operate in French or English.[^cite_note-361] The top-ranking universities in the country are the [American University of Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_University_of_Beirut) (#2 in the Middle East as of 2022 and #226 worldwide),[^cite_note-Top_Universities-2022-362] [University of Balamand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Balamand) (#17 in the region and #802–850 worldwide),[^cite_note-Top_Universities-2022-362] [Lebanese American University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_American_University) (#17 in the region and #501 worldwide),[^cite_note-Top_Universities-2022-362] [Université Saint Joseph de Beyrouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_Saint_Joseph_de_Beyrouth) (#2 in Lebanon and #631–640 worldwide),[^cite_note-363] [Université Libanaise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universit%C3%A9_Libanaise) (#577 worldwide) and [Holy Spirit University of Kaslik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Spirit_University_of_Kaslik) (#600s worldwide as of 2020).[^cite_note-364] [Notre Dame University-Louaize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre_Dame_University%E2%80%93Louaize) (NDU) (#701 as of 2021).[^cite_note-365]&#x20;

### Health

Main article: [Health in Lebanon](35327203.md)



Belluve Medical Center

In 2010, spending on healthcare accounted for 7.03% of Lebanon's GDP. In 2009, there were 31.29 physicians and 19.71 nurses per 10,000 inhabitants.[^cite_note-366] The life expectancy at birth was 72.59 years in 2011, or 70.48 years for males and 74.80 years for females.[^cite_note-367] By the end of the civil war, only one-third of Lebanon's public hospitals were operational, each with an average of 20 beds. By 2009, Lebanon had 28 public hospitals, with a total of 2,550 beds.[^cite_note-healthref-368]&#x20;

At public hospitals, hospitalized uninsured patients pay 5% of the bill, in comparison with 15% in private hospitals, with the Ministry of Public Health reimbursing the remainder.[^cite_note-healthref-368] The Ministry of Public Health contracts with 138 private hospitals and 25 public hospitals.[^cite_note-statbul11-369]&#x20;

In 2011, there were 236,643 subsidized admissions to hospitals; 164,244 in private hospitals, and 72,399 in public hospitals. More patients visit private hospitals than public hospitals, because the private beds supply is higher.[^cite_note-statbul11-369] In 2017, the top 10 leading causes of reported hospital deaths were: malignant neoplasm of bronchus or lung (4.6%), Acute [myocardial infarction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myocardial_infarction) (3%), [pneumonia](52135.md) (2.2%), exposure to unspecified factor, unspecified place (2.1%), [acute kidney injury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_kidney_injury) (1.4%), intra-cerebral hemorrhage (1.2%), malignant neoplasm of colon (1.2%), malignant neoplasm of pancreas (1.1%), malignant neoplasm of prostate (1.1%), malignant neoplasm of bladder (0.8%).[^cite_note-370]&#x20;

Recently, there has been an increase in foodborne illnesses in Lebanon. This has raised public awareness on the importance of food safety, including in the realms of food storage, preservation, and preparation. More restaurants are seeking information and compliance with [International Organization for Standardization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Organization_for_Standardization).[^cite_note-kebabs-371]&#x20;

The [Asfouriyeh Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asfouriyeh_Hospital), founded in 1896 in Lebanon, is considered the first modern mental health hospital in the Middle East. The devastating impact of the [Lebanese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Civil_War) led to the hospital's closure in 1982.[^cite_note-MIT-2023-372]&#x20;

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Lebanon)



The [Temple of Bacchus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_of_Bacchus) is considered among the best preserved [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Empire) temples in the world, c. 150 AD.

The culture of Lebanon reflects the legacy of various civilizations spanning thousands of years. Originally home to the [Canaanite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canaan)-[Phoenicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenicia), and then conquered and occupied by the [Assyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyria), the [Persians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persia), the [Greeks](42056.md), the [Romans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome), the Arabs, the Crusaders, the [Ottoman Turks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Turks) and most recently the French, Lebanese culture has over the millennia evolved by borrowing from all of these groups.[^cite_note-StokesJamie-373]&#x20;

Lebanon's diverse population, composed of different ethnic and religious groups, has further contributed to the country's festivals, musical styles and literature as well as cuisine. Despite the ethnic, linguistic, religious and denominational diversity of the Lebanese, they "share an almost common culture".[^cite_note-StokesJamie-373] [Lebanese Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Arabic) is universally spoken while food, music, and literature are deep-rooted "in wider Mediterranean and Levantine norms".[^cite_note-StokesJamie-373]&#x20;

### Arts

In visual arts, [Moustafa Farroukh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moustafa_Farroukh) was among Lebanon's most prominent painters of the 20th century. Formally trained in Rome and Paris, he exhibited in venues from Paris to New York to Beirut over his career.[^cite_note-374] Many more contemporary artists are active, such as [Walid Raad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walid_Raad), a contemporary media artist residing in New York.[^cite_note-375] In the field of photography, the [Arab Image Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Image_Foundation) has a collection of over 400,000 photographs from Lebanon and the Middle East. The photographs can be viewed in a research center and various events and publications have been produced in Lebanon and worldwide to promote the collection.&#x20;

### Literature

In literature, [Kahlil Gibran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kahlil_Gibran) is the third best-selling poet of all time, behind [Shakespeare](32897.md) and [Laozi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laozi).[^cite_note-376] He is particularly known for his 1923 book *[The Prophet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Prophet_\(book\))*, which has been translated into over twenty different languages.[^cite_note-377] [Ameen Rihani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ameen_Rihani) was a major figure in the mahjar literary movement developed by Arab emigrants in North America, and an early theorist of [Arab nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_nationalism). [Mikhail Naimy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Naimy) is widely recognized as among the most important figures in modern Arabic letters and among the most important spiritual writers of the 20th century. Several contemporary Lebanese writers have also achieved international success; including [Elias Khoury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Khoury), [Amin Maalouf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amin_Maalouf), [Hanan al-Shaykh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanan_al-Shaykh), and [Georges Schéhadé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Sch%C3%A9had%C3%A9).&#x20;

### Music

Main article: [Music of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Lebanon)



[Fairuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairuz)

While traditional folk music remains popular in Lebanon, modern music reconciling Western and traditional Arabic styles, pop, and [fusion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fusion_\(music\)) are rapidly advancing in popularity.[^cite_note-Traditional-378] Lebanese artists like [Fairuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairuz), [Majida El Roumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majida_El_Roumi), [Wadih El Safi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wadih_El_Safi), [Sabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_\(singer\)), [Julia Boutros](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Boutros) or [Najwa Karam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najwa_Karam) are widely known and appreciated in Lebanon and in the Arab world. Radio stations feature a variety of music, including traditional Lebanese, classical Arabic, Armenian[^cite_note-379] and modern French, English, American, and [Latin](18524.md) tunes.[^cite_note-380]&#x20;

### Media and cinema

The [cinema of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Lebanon), according to film critic and historian, Roy Armes, was the only cinema in the Arabic-speaking region, besides the dominant [Egyptian cinema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Egypt),[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] that could amount to a national cinema.[^cite_note-383] Cinema in Lebanon has been in existence since the 1920s. Lebanon has produced over 500 films, with many films including Egyptian filmmakers and film stars.[^cite_note-384]&#x20;

The [media of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_of_Lebanon) is a regional center of production and the most liberal and free in the Arab world.[^cite_note-385] According to Press freedom's [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md), "the media have more freedom in Lebanon than in any other Arab country".[^cite_note-BBC-2011-26] Despite its small population and geographic size, Lebanon plays an influential role in the production of information in the Arab world and is "at the core of a regional media network with global implications".[^cite_note-b1-386]&#x20;

### Holidays and festivals

Main article: [Public holidays in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Lebanon)

Lebanon celebrates national and both [Christian](4796145.md) and [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Lebanon) holidays. Christian holidays are celebrated following both the [Gregorian calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregorian_calendar) and [Julian calendar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julian_calendar). [Greek Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Christianity_in_Lebanon) (with the exception of Easter), [Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_Lebanon), [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_Lebanon), and [Melkite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melkite_Christianity_in_Lebanon) Christians follow the Gregorian Calendar and thus celebrate Christmas on 25 December. Armenian [Apostolic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_Apostolic_Church) Christians celebrate Christmas on 6 January, as they follow the Julian Calendar.[^cite_note-Festivals-387]&#x20;

Muslim holidays are followed based on the Islamic lunar calendar. Muslim holidays that are celebrated include Eid al-Fitr (the three-day feast at the end of the Ramadan month), Eid al-Adha (The Feast of the Sacrifice) which is celebrated during the annual pilgrimage to Mecca and also celebrates Abraham's willingness to sacrifice his son to God, the Birth of the Islamic prophet [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad), and Ashura (the Shiite Day of Mourning). Lebanon's National Holidays include Workers Day, Independence Day, and Martyrs Day. Music festivals, often hosted at historical sites, are a customary element of Lebanese culture.[^cite_note-Festivals-387]&#x20;

Among the most famous are [Baalbeck International Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baalbeck_International_Festival), [Byblos International Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byblos_International_Festival), [Beiteddine International Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beiteddine_Festival), Jounieh International Festival, Broumana Festival, Batroun International Festival, Ehmej Festival, Dhour Chwer Festival and Tyr Festival.[^cite_note-Festivals-387][^cite_note-388] These festivals are promoted by Lebanon's [Ministry of Tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Tourism_\(Lebanon\)). Lebanon hosts about 15 concerts from international performers each year, ranking 1st for nightlife in the Middle East, and 6th worldwide.[^cite_note-389]&#x20;

### Cuisine

Main article: [Lebanese cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_cuisine)

Lebanese cuisine is similar to those of many countries in the [Eastern Mediterranean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Mediterranean), such as Syria, Turkey, Greece, and Cyprus. The Lebanese national dishes are the [kibbe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kibbe), a meat pie made from finely minced [lamb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_and_mutton) and [burghul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burghul) (cracked [wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wheat)), and the [tabbouleh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabbouleh), a salad made from [parsley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parsley), [tomatoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato), and [burghul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burghul) wheat. Lebanese restaurant meals begin with a wide array of [mezze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mezze) – small savoury dishes, such as dips, salads, and pastries. The mezze are typically followed by a selection of grilled [meat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meat) or [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish). In general, meals are finished with [Arabic coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_coffee) and fresh [fruit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fruit), though sometimes a selection of traditional sweets will be offered as well.&#x20;

### Sports

Main article: [Sport in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Lebanon)



[Camille Chamoun Sports City Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camille_Chamoun_Sports_City_Stadium) in [Beirut](37428.md)

Lebanon has six [ski resorts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skiing_in_Lebanon). The country's unique geography makes it possible to go skiing in the morning and swimming in the [Mediterranean Sea](19006.md) in the afternoon.[^cite_note-Aikman2009-390] At the competitive level, basketball and [football](10568.md) are among Lebanon's most popular sports. [Canoeing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canoeing), [cycling](5931.md), [rafting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafting), [climbing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climbing), swimming, sailing and [caving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caving) are among the other common leisure sports in Lebanon. The [Beirut Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beirut_Marathon) is held every fall, drawing top runners from Lebanon and abroad.[^cite_note-391]&#x20;

[Rugby league](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_league_in_Lebanon) is a relatively new but growing sport in Lebanon. The [Lebanon national rugby league team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_national_rugby_league_team) participated in the [2000 Rugby League World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Rugby_League_World_Cup),[^cite_note-392] and narrowly missed qualification for the [2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_Rugby_League_World_Cup)[^cite_note-393] and [2013](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Rugby_League_World_Cup) tournaments.[^cite_note-394] They qualified again for the [2017 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_Rugby_League_World_Cup) reaching the quarter-finals, narrowly losing 24–22 to [Tonga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonga_national_rugby_league_team).[^cite_note-395] This ensured qualification for [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Rugby_League_World_Cup). Their 2021 quarterfinal was not as competitive, losing 48–4 to eventual champions [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australia_national_rugby_league_team).[^cite_note-396] Lebanon also took part in the [2009 European Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_European_Cup) where, after narrowly failing to qualify for the final, the team defeated [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland_national_rugby_league_team) to finish 3rd in the tournament.[^cite_note-397]&#x20;



[Al Ansar FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Ansar_FC) in [Beirut](37428.md)

Lebanon participates in [basketball](3921.md). The [Lebanese National Team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_men%27s_national_basketball_team) qualified for the [FIBA World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_World_Championship) three times in a row.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399] Dominant basketball teams are [Sporting Al Riyadi Beirut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sporting_Al_Riyadi_Beirut),[^cite_note-400] who are the Arab and Asian champions, and [Club Sagesse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sagesse_SC_\(basketball\)), who have earned the Asian and Arab championships.&#x20;

[Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Lebanon) is also among the more popular sports in the country. The top football league is the [Lebanese Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Premier_League), whose most successful clubs are [Al Ansar FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Ansar_FC) and [Nejmeh SC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nejmeh_SC). In recent years, Lebanon has hosted the [AFC Asian Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFC_Asian_Cup)[^cite_note-401] and the [Pan Arab Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_Arab_Games).[^cite_note-402] Lebanon hosted the [2009 Jeux de la Francophonie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Jeux_de_la_Francophonie),[^cite_note-403] and [have participated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_at_the_Olympics) in every [Olympic Games](22576.md) since its independence, winning a total of four medals.[^cite_note-404] Water sports in Lebanon have been very active in recent years. Since 2012 and with the emergence of the Lebanon Water Festival NGO, more emphasis has been placed on those sports, and Lebanon has been pushed forward as a water sport destination internationally.[^cite_note-405] They host different contests and water show sports that encourage their fans to participate and win big.[^cite_note-406]&#x20;

[^cite_note-Rooke-64]: &#x20;Tetz Rooke (2013). ["Writing the Boundary: "Khitat al-Shăm" by Muhammad Kurd ʹAli"](https://books.google.com/books?id=ykTYAQAAQBAJ\&pg=PA178). In Hiroyuki (ed.). *Concept Of Territory In Islamic Thought*. Routledge. p. 178. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-1-136-18453-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-1-136-18453-6). His \[([Thongchai Winichakul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thongchai_Winichakul)'s)] study shows that the modern map in some cases predicted the nation instead of just recording it; rather than describing existing borders it created the reality it was assumed to depict. The power of the map over the mind was great:"\[H]ow could a nation resist being found if a nineteenth-century map had predicted it?" In the Middle East, Lebanon seems to offer a corresponding example. When the idea of a Greater Lebanon in 1908 was put forward in a book by Bulus Nujaym, a Lebanese Maronite writing under the pseudonym of M. Jouplain, he suggested that the natural boundaries of Lebanon were exactly the same as drawn in the 1861 and 1863 staff maps of the French military expedition to Syria, maps that added territories on the northern, eastern and southern borders, plus the city of Beirut, to the Mutasarrifiyya of Mount Lebanon. In this case, too, the prior existence of a European military map seems to have created a fact on the ground.
